    # GPU-RESIDENT DECODE (#506) — DEFAULT OFF. "nvdec_resident" moves decode+resize+normalize
    # onto the GPU with no host round-trip: PyNvVideoCodec NVDEC decode → affine `grid_sample`
    # resize replicating WASB's cv2.warpAffine EXACTLY (the #506 lesson: a naive F.interpolate
    # silently over-detects — F1 0.58→0.07 — and only shows on hard clips) → GPU ImageNet
    # normalize → HRNet. Validated on a real L4 (GX010128, SERVED preset, 2026-07-06): ~1.7×
    # fps (35.8→60.2), GPU util 47→79% / CPU 62→35%, F1 0.574 ≈ baseline 0.582. Native (Linux)
    # runner only — needs the torch-2.x + PyNvVideoCodec WASB env (deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile)
    # with libnvcuvid/libnvidia-encode present, and device=cuda; the WSL runner ignores it.
130→    # Flip gate: the full 15-clip golden set at the SERVED preset (GX010128 must pass).
    decode_backend: Literal["cpu", "nvdec_resident"] = "cpu"

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _nvdec_resident_requires_cuda(self) -> "WasbIndexConfig":
        # NVDEC decode is CUDA-only; reject the contradictory combo at config load instead of
        # failing deep inside a paid GPU run. "auto" is allowed here — the native runner's
        # healthcheck hard-fails if auto resolves to cpu with this backend on.
        if self.decode_backend == "nvdec_resident" and self.device not in ("cuda", "auto"):
            raise ValueError(
140→                f"indexing.wasb.decode_backend='nvdec_resident' requires device 'cuda' or "
                f"'auto' (got {self.device!r}) — NVDEC decode runs on the GPU."
            )
        return self

    @field_validator("batch_size")
    @classmethod
    def _batch_size_positive(cls, v: Optional[int]) -> Optional[int]:
        # A DataLoader batch must be >=1; reject at config time so a typo can't reach
        # `wasb_infer.py --batch-size` as an invalid value (the runner only passes the flag
150→        # when truthy, so 0 stays "off/parity-default").
        if v is not None and v < 1:
            raise ValueError(f"indexing.wasb.batch_size must be >= 1 when set (got {v})")
        return v

    @field_validator("prefetch_batches")
    @classmethod
    def _prefetch_batches_non_negative(cls, v: Optional[int]) -> Optional[int]:
        # 0 = off (the default). Reject negatives: the runner passes any truthy value as
        # `--prefetch-batches`, and `_PrefetchIter` clamps depth with max(1, int(depth)), so a
160→        # negative would SILENTLY enable prefetch at depth 1 instead of failing loudly.
        if v is not None and v < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"indexing.wasb.prefetch_batches must be >= 0 when set (got {v})"
            )
        return v


class FusionConfig(BaseModel):
    """Config for the multi-signal `fusion_hybrid` segmenter (MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN P2).
170→
    Every default reproduces control behaviour: the fusion segmenter persists the
    shuttle net-crossing count (Gate A signal) and — only when ``cue_flags['person']`` is
    true — the person-cue features, but it does NOT gate the served handoff unless a
    threshold is raised (``min_crossings``/``min_players`` default 0 = OFF). The court mask
    is optional: ``court_polygon=None`` ⇒ Gate B counts every detection (player-count-only);
    supplied ⇒ off-court persons (spectators / adjacent courts) are excluded.
    """

    # Which trajectory substrate seeds the windows (shuttle stays primary).
180→    substrate: Literal["wasb", "tracknet"] = "wasb"
    # Windowing velocity threshold for the fusion family (the engine reads
    # indexing.<family>.velocity_threshold). Default matches the substrates' 0.02; set
    # equal to indexing.wasb.velocity_threshold to A/B fusion against a tuned wasb run.
    velocity_threshold: float = 0.02
    # Per-cue enable flags, e.g. {"person": true}. Person cue is OFF unless explicitly
    # enabled — so the default path never imports torch / touches the GPU.
    cue_flags: dict[str, bool] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    # Served Gate A: drop windows whose shuttle net-crossings < this from the AI handoff.
    # 0 = OFF (no gating; persisted candidates are always the full superset for offline re-scoring).
190→    min_crossings: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    # Served Gate B: when the person cue is on, drop windows with median in-court players
    # < this. 0 = OFF.
    min_players: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    # Optional court mask as normalised 0-1 [[x, y], ...] polygon. None = no mask.
    court_polygon: Optional[list[list[float]]] = None
    # Net line for the person two-sided-motion split (person features only — the shuttle
    # net-crossing gate keeps rally_gate's camera-agnostic auto-estimate).
    net_axis: Literal["x", "y"] = "y"
    net_position: float = Field(default=0.5, ge=0.0, le=1.0)

    def _chunk_step_must_be_positive(self) -> "IndexingConfig":
        # The chunked segmenters advance by (chunk_duration - chunk_overlap); a non-positive
        # step makes `while t < duration: t += step` loop forever, growing memory before any
        # GPU work. Reject the bad config at load time instead.
        if self.chunk_overlap >= self.chunk_duration:

                f"({self.chunk_duration}); otherwise chunking never advances."

        return self

### 188. assistant

**Done.** Sampling is no longer driven by code constants.

### Changes made

1. **New config knobs** (in `backend/config/models.py`):

   - `indexing.detector_progress_sample_sec` (default `15.0`)
     - Controls the rate at which we perform the *expensive* sidecar actions in the worker:
       - live `storage.put` of `..._detector_progress.json` (what the control plane polls during a cloud/L4 run)
       - `RunTelemetry.snapshot(...)` (the one that calls `nvidia-smi` + rewrites the JSONL)
     - The **local** sidecar file is still written on *every* callback (cheap, so the latest value is always present in scratch).
     - `<= 0` disables sampling (every callback does the work).

   - `indexing.wasb.log_every_batches` (default `50`)
     - Controls the *emission* rate of the `"done/total (pct%) ..."` lines from `wasb_infer.py` that the native runner turns into progress callbacks.
     - This was previously a hard-coded default inside `wasb_infer`.

2. **Wiring**:
   - Added to `WasbIndexConfig` + `NativeWasbConfig`.
   - `NativeWasbRunner` now forwards `--log-every-batches` when building the argv (matching the existing CLI flag).
   - In `backend/worker/__main__.py:cmd_infer`, the `_progress` closure now does proper time-based sampling using the value from `config.indexing.detector_progress_sample_sec`.
   - `_apply_overrides` (and therefore `--set indexing.detector_progress_sample_sec=5` and `--set indexing.wasb.log_every_batches=20`) works unchanged.

3. **Behaviour**:
   - Local file write + `print` → always latest (cheap).
   - Remote put + telemetry snapshot → rate-limited.
   - Everything stays best-effort / wrapped in `try/except`.
   - Works for both direct CLI and dispatched L4 runs.

### Usage examples

```json
{
  "indexing": {
    "detector_progress_sample_sec": 10.0,
    "wasb": {
      "log_every_batches": 25
    }
  }
}
```

Or via the worker:

```bash
python -m backend.worker infer ... \
  --set indexing.detector_progress_sample_sec=5 \
  --set indexing.wasb.log_every_batches=20
```

Fast gates (Ruff + Mypy + import smoke) are still clean after the changes.

The two rates (emission from the detector + action sampling on the receiver) are now proper configuration surface instead of magic numbers. Let me know if you want a validator, different default, docs update, or similar sampling on the CP poll side.

def test_cmd_train_detector_joins_dated_labels_to_bare_videos(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Regression (DATA_IN_GCS §7b blocker #1): canonical labels carry an ISO-date prefix
    (labels/<date>_<name>.rallies.csv) while videos are bare (videos/<name>.mp4). The label-derived
490→    clip name must strip the date so the video join succeeds — otherwise every clip is skipped and
    train aborts with <2 videos."""
    import os
    import subprocess

    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL", "stub")  # CPU stub runner (no GPU/WSL)
    monkeypatch.setattr(wmain, "_probe_video_fps_width", lambda p: (30.0, 1280.0))
    corpus = tmp_path / "corpus"
    labels_dir = corpus / "labels"

### 213. assistant

**Yes — I added extra tests for the configurable sampling.**

Here's what was added specifically for the new sampling/configurability work (on top of the earlier progress callback test):

### New tests

1. **`test_detector_progress_sampling_config()`** in [tests/test_config.py](/tests/test_config.py)
   - Verifies the new fields have the right defaults.
   - Tests constructor/dict overrides.
   - Tests round-trip via JSON file load (the way real `config.json` + `--set` work).

2. **`test_run_predict_passes_log_every_batches_for_progress_sampling()`** in [tests/test_native_wasb_runner.py](/tests/test_native_wasb_runner.py)
   - Confirms that `log_every_batches` (the emission rate for the `%` lines) flows from config into the native `wasb_infer.py` argv.
   - Covers both explicit override and default.

3. **`test_cmd_infer_respects_detector_progress_sample_config()`** in [tests/test_worker.py](/tests/test_worker.py)
   - Exercises the full `cmd_infer` path with `--set indexing.detector_progress_sample_sec=...` (both small and large values).
   - Uses a fake segmenter that emits detector progress and verifies the run stays stable when the sampling knob is set.

### Context on coverage

- The core "progress callback + Popen streaming" behavior already had `test_run_predict_streams_and_calls_progress_on_pct_lines` (added earlier in this thread).
- These new tests specifically target the **configurability** part (the thing that was previously a hard-coded constant / magic number).
- Fast gates (Ruff + Mypy + import smoke) still pass cleanly after the additions.

Would you like a more rigorous test for the actual time-based throttling logic inside `_progress` (e.g. using a fake clock to assert exact put/snapshot counts)? I kept the worker test relatively lightweight to avoid flakiness, but we can make it stricter if needed.
